Repeated 'WebSocket ... 429' console lines (one default Nostr relay rate limiting us) and 'player ... ready' lines are left out.

=== stress.mjs notes added by hand (runs that produced no clean report; numbers copied from the console of those runs, the files of the runs were lost in a restart)
Harness: bots are 8 per browser page (one iframe each), 5 local relays on 127.0.0.1 with a throw-away TLS certificate, Chrome with --ignore-certificate-errors and every name except 127.0.0.1 refused. Isolation self-check at the start of every local run: play stats service reachable false, public relay host reachable false, STUN server-reflexive candidates 0. Network log of the local runs: websocket hosts only 127.0.0.1:<port>, http hosts only 127.0.0.1, refused lookups none.
D 64 (rolling join, 3 s apart, 2 real + 62 bots): not a clean result. The harness lost contact with its pages for 134 s (longest gap between samples), 9 players never connected, no final state. In an earlier run of the same scenario, made before every bot page had a browser context of its own, 13 of 64 players (the last 13 to start) never got a single relay socket, because Chrome had stopped opening WebSockets at about 300 for the context (measured separately: 150 + 150 sockets opened, a third batch of 150 got 3). That one was a harness limit, not the game.
C 32 and C 64, E 32, E 64, B 32, B 64: started, not completed. C 32 (wave leaving) ran several times (half of the players teleporting to places of their own, and half driving off at 40 m/s): every time the pages stopped answering about 30 s after the wave began (a sample of 32 pages took 20 s, then 110 s between samples), once a bot page crashed ("detached frame"), in the teleport run the relays saw about 16000 frames/s (busiest relay 3227/s, peak second 12780 frames out) and Chrome grew to 6.5 GB. The runs were stopped by hand or by the watchdog. E 32 was killed by the watchdog (pages not answering). C 64, E 64, B 32 and B 64 were not run to the end (time, and the Mac was restarted).
H 41 (maxp 40) desktop and phone profile: killed by the watchdog. In the desktop run the sample at t = 148 s took 20.6 s (pages not answering), memory free 49 %. No report was written for either.
Public relay run (P 4, 1 real page + 3 bots, the one allowed run): websocket hosts of that run were the five pinned relays (nostr.stakey.net, nos.lol, nostr.chaima.info, nostr.christiansass.de, relay.bitmacro.cloud), http hosts only 127.0.0.1 (the game files), nothing went to the play stats service. The bots connected to each other 599 to 717 ms after their start, against 280 to 370 ms on the local relays at about the same size.
Experiment on the cause of the churn in B (8 bots, no real page, spread over the city, same seed, 110 s, steady window 50 s, same Mac, back to back). 'plain' is the shipped src/p2p/p2p.js, 'patched' is the bots' copy with g.anon = 0 in evaluate() instead of Math.max(0, r.peers.size - g.who.size):
  plain:   room joins 37.0 per player per minute, connection events (peerJoin) 409 per player per minute, relays 3744 frames/s in total, busiest relay 751 frames/s, 166 join errors.
  patched: room joins 5.6 per player per minute, connection events 40 per player per minute, relays 349 frames/s in total, busiest relay 70.5 frames/s, 157 join errors.
